<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom"><I>%&nbsp;</I></TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. <B>EBITDA</B> = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right
of use assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. <B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items.
<B>Net Leverage Ratio </B>= Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. <B>FCF</B> = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). <B>Fuels = </B>diesel +
gasoline + jet fuel. <B>R&amp;M </B>is refining and marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro businesses. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>1. MAIN HIGHLIGHTS </B></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> amounted to US$4,058&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-18%</FONT> vs. 2022), on the back of lower domestic fuel prices in
dollar terms, a downward trend in the prices of other refined products and higher OPEX, partially offset by the expansion in total production and processing levels and new FX regime for exporters. 4Q23 contributed with an Adj. EBITDA of
US$1,082&nbsp;million (+17% q/q), primarily on the back of said exports&#146; FX regime and higher local fuels prices in dollars terms, partially compensated by seasonal lower natural gas sales. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total hydrocarbon production</B> averaged 514 Kboe/d in 2023 (+2% vs. 2022) and 511 Kboe/d in 4Q23 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q but +2%
y/y), mainly boosted by the expansion in crude oil production, while natural gas production declined by 3% in 2023 driven by lower demand. 46% of 2023 total production was shale.<B> Shale oil production </B>continued delivering an impressive growth
rate of 27% in 2023, reaching 109 Kbbl/d in 4Q23 (+28% y/y), while <B>Medanito oil exports to Chile</B> stabilized in 19 Kbbl/d. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total P1 reserves</B>
reached 1.1&nbsp;billion BOE by <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-2023</FONT> <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-10%</FONT> vs. 2022), largely driven by the drop of 26% in conventional reserves, whereas shale reserves, 71% of the total, remained
stable. The general reserve replacement ratio (RRR) stood at 0.4x, while shale RRR was 1.0x. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuel&#146;s demand </B>reached historical records
during 2023, standing 3% above 2022 levels, supplied by 3% higher <B>processing levels at our refineries</B> and higher fuels imports, which jumped 8% against 2022, representing 11% of total local fuels sales. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>totaled US$5,684&nbsp;million in 2023 (+36% y/y), exceeding the initial target of the year as a result of higher costs in dollar terms, achieving
the activity originally planned. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Free cash flow</B> totaled a negative US$740&nbsp;million in 2023 and slightly negative at US$60&nbsp;million in 4Q23,
the latter backed on the sequential EBITDA expansion and positive working capital variations, partly offset by the deployment of the CAPEX Plan, taking our <B>net debt</B> to US$6.8&nbsp;billion and <B>net leverage ratio</B> to 1.7x. </P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>

<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">1</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">1 ADR = 1 share. Total issued capital stock amounted to 393,312,793 shares as of
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-2023</FONT> (51% Argentina Government; 35% NYSE and 14% ByMA). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net Revenues</B> totaled US$17.3&nbsp;billion in 2023, decreasing 8% y/y mainly driven by lower prices of fuels, other
refined and petrochemical products, and fertilizers, as well as the impact of a severe drought in the first half of the year that particularly affected exports of grain and flours, partially offset by the resumption of Medanito crude oil exports to
Chile and increased local demand of fuels, particularly gasoline. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Revenues in 4Q contracted 7% sequentially mainly due to lower seasonal natural gas sales
and the impact of the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December</FONT> discrete devaluation on subsidiaries with Argentine Peso as the functional currency<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">2</SUP>, partially offset by higher local
fuels prices in dollar terms and a record high gasoline demand. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:8pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$20) million in 4Q22, US$32&nbsp;million for 3Q23, (US$29) million for 4Q23,
US$245&nbsp;million for 2022 and (US$62) million for 2023. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> totaled US$6.0&nbsp;billion in 2023 (+13% y/y), mainly explained by a negative
evolution of macroeconomic variables along the year, such as inflation, wages, and currency devaluation, coupled with an overall activity increase across all businesses, on the back of higher O&amp;G production, fuels demand and processing levels.
It is noteworthy that in 4Q23, OPEX dropped 8% q/q, primarily due to the impact of the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December</FONT> discrete devaluation on subsidiaries with Argentine Peso as the functional currency. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total Purchases and Stock Variations</B> amounted to US$5.2&nbsp;billion <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-14%</FONT> y/y) mainly due to lower agro
purchases (grain and flours, as well as fertilizers) aligned with the lower demand previously described, reduced availability of biodiesel and the impact of downward trend in international prices on imported fuels. This was partly offset by higher
crude oil purchases, in line with better processing levels and a negative stock variation as a result of lower replacement cost of our inventories. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:8pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">2</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">The financial information of our subsidiaries, having the Argentine Peso as functional currency, is adjusted
for inflation, corresponding to a hyperinflationary economy in accordance with IAS guidelines, and consolidated using the exchange rate for the end of the period. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">3 </P>

<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g747204g07h78.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">A <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-recurring</FONT><B> impairment </B>charge of US$2,288&nbsp;million was
recorded by the company in 2023, mainly led an impairment of Property, Plant and Equipment charge accounted in 4Q23 of US$ 1,782&nbsp;million. This charge is a result of the revision of recoverable value of conventional mature fields on the back of
a new disposal strategy approved by the Board of Directors on February&nbsp;29, 2024, which requires a future reclassification to &#147;held for sale assets&#148;, according to the applicable regulation (IFRS). </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net financial results</B> represented a US$897&nbsp;million gain in 2023, compared to a US$128&nbsp;million gain in 2022,
mainly explained by the impact of the devaluation on the Argentine peso net monetary-liability position, partially offset by higher debt interests. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The
<B>income tax charge</B> amounted to US$1,020&nbsp;million in 2023 (+24% y/y), mainly as a result of the local currency devaluation outpacing inflation in cumulative terms for the year, resulting in an increase in deferred tax liabilities. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Thus, <B>net result</B> reached a negative mark of US$1,277&nbsp;million, compared to a gain of US$2,234&nbsp;million in 2022.<B> </B> </P>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom"><B><I>%</I></B>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
<TR STYLE="font-size:1px; ">
<TD VALIGN="bottom"></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>

<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>

<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>

<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$7.4&nbsp;billion in 2023 (similar to 2022), mainly driven by higher crude oil volume sold (+6%),
mostly offset by reduced crude oil price <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%),</FONT> which was particularly affected by the agreement between local refineries, upstream companies and the Secretary of Energy during the 2<SUP
STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">nd</SUP> half of the year, and to a lesser extent, the decline in natural gas demand <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%).</FONT> </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">5 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g747204g07h78.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting cost</B> reached US$15.4/BOE in 2023 (+16% y/y), primarily due to cost pressures in local
currency, as the inflation surpassed the FX devaluation, as well as higher unconventional activities and maintenance costs, partly offset by the expanded crude oil production. Our conventional blocks averaged US$25.0/BOE (+20% y/y), while
unconventional lifting cost was US$5.3/BOE (+33% y/y). Zooming in, lifting cost of our shale core hub blocks, on a gross basis, stood at US$4.1/BOE (+13% y/y), a very competitive rate despite macro context. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>and other taxes averaged US$6.4/BOE<B> </B>in 2023 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> y/y), as a result of said decline in natural
gas demand and lower hydrocarbon realization prices, partially offset by the growing crude oil output. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Lastly, it is worth noting that in 2023 the
Company recorded a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-recurring</FONT> <B>impairment charge</B> of US$2.3 billion: US$1.8&nbsp;billion in 4Q23 from the revision of recoverable value of conventional mature fields on the back of a new disposal
strategy and US$0.5&nbsp;billion in 3Q23 in our natural gas assets due to lower long-term expected natural gas prices as a result of increased competition and potential oversupply in the domestic market in coming years. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled US$2.8&nbsp;billion in 2023 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-9%</FONT> y/y), driven by higher OPEX, lower local crude oil
price and reduced natural gas demand, partially offset by growing oil production and new FX regime for exporters. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled
US$4.3&nbsp;billion (+35% y/y), where 65% was allocated to drilling and workover activities, 29% to new facilities or the expansion of existing ones, and 6% to exploration and other upstream activities. 67% of total capex was deployed within the
unconventional areas. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Drilling and workover activities</B> showed a positive trend, completing a total of 318 new wells in our operated blocks: 161
horizontal shale (138 oil and 23 gas) and 157 conventional, reaching the highest number of horizontal shale wells completed in the last four years. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In
terms of <B>efficiencies within our shale operations</B>, in 2023 the company continued setting new records on drilling and fracking performance, averaging 274 meters per day in drilling and over 200 stages per set per month on fracking, increasing
by 9% and 6%, respectively vs. 2022. Moreover, in November 2023, we achieved the highest drilling speed for one slim-design well in Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block, reaching 473 meters per day for a well of over 2,500 meters of horizontal length,
which was fully drilled in 12 days. On the other hand, in November 2023, we successfully tested two dual-fuel engines in Bandurria Sur block for fracking pumps, replacing up to 60% of diesel consumption by natural gas, aiming at optimizing the use
of flared gas as fuel. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our exploratory strategy, during 2023, the company was awarded with two new shale exploratory concessions in Palermo
Aike and continued progressing in our <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">off-shore</FONT> strategy, expecting to drill the first ultra-deep-water exploratory well of Argentina by 2024. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">6 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> in 2023 increased 7% y/y, on the back of a solid growth of shale (+27%), while conventional
dropped just 2%, as the natural decline of mature fields was mostly offset by tertiary production, which increased 38% y/y. 70% of total tertiary production came from Manantiales Behr block. In Q4, oil production recorded a sequential increase of
almost 8%, on the back of stable conventional production and a new remarkable expansion of 19% on shale production. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production</B> in 2023
decreased 3% y/y, driven by a contraction in demand, affecting conventional, partially offset by 8% expansion in shale.<B> NGL production</B> grew 3% y/y, mainly due to the new facilities connected between Tratay&eacute;n and our subsidiary Mega.
</P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">7 </P>

</DIV></Center>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Proved reserves (P1)</B> closed in 2023 at 1,072 Mboe <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-10%</FONT> y/y), mainly driven by
a large decrease of 26% in conventional fields while shale remained almost flat. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The addition of proved (developed and undeveloped) hydrocarbon reserves
reached 72&nbsp;million barrels of oil equivalent mainly driven by the progressive developments of our unconventional operations and the effect of variations in oil and gas prices and costs. This result was achieved through the incorporation of
41&nbsp;million barrels of liquids and 31 MBOE corresponding to gas reserves. Considering that reserves incorporated during the year were below 2023 total production (187 MBoe), the reserve replacement ratio (&#147;RRR&#148;) reached 0.4x (0.3x oil
and 0.4x gas) with 5.7 years of reserve life. Shale reserves represented 71% of the total P1 reserves, averaging 8.8 years and RRR of 1.0x. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The following
is a breakdown of the main variations of the addition of P1 reserves by basin: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="3%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B></B>Neuquina Basin: mainly unconventional additions from La Amarga Chica and Bandurria Sur blocks for oil and
Aguada Pichana Oeste and La Calera for gas. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

development of the tertiary recovery technics. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.2 DOWNSTREAM </B></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of 4Q23, the Company has decided to merge the Industrialization and Commercialization segments into a single Downstream segment. In addition, sales of
natural gas were reallocated to the Gas and Power segment, restating comparative information corresponding to the period from 1Q22 to 3Q23. This reclassification is aligned with the new management structure and aims at simplifying the business
reporting and maximizing synergies. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$73) million in 4Q22, US$(71) million for 3Q23, US$37&nbsp;million for 4Q23,
US$247&nbsp;million for 2022 and (US$164)&nbsp;million for 2023. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled almost US$15&nbsp;billion in 2023
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-7%</FONT> y/y), mainly due to a decline in local prices of fuels, other refined and petrochemical products, and fertilizers, in addition to lower exported volumes of grain and flours (affected by a severe drought
recorded in Argentina during the first half of the year), partially offset by the resumption of Medanito crude oil exports to Chile and increased local demand of fuels, particularly gasoline. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Refining and logistics costs </B>increased 13% y/y in 2023 mainly driven by higher costs of transportation, materials, and wages, primarily on the back of
the evolution of local macro variables and increased demand and crude oil processed. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fuel imports</B> decreased by 12% y/y in 2023, on the back of
lower international prices <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-18%</FONT> vs. 2022), partially offset by an increase of 8% in imported volumes. It is worth mentioning that in 4Q23 fuels imported volumes increased by 173% q/q in order to supply the
record high local fuels demand and compensate the programmed stoppage of a topping unit at La Plata refinery. However, annual fuel imported volumes remained stable at ordinary levels, representing 11% of our local fuels demand. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases</B> (including intersegment) expanded by 3% y/y in 2023, mainly driven by a 6% increase in volumes (higher processing levels),
partially offset by a 3% contraction in crude oil prices. <B>Biofuel purchases</B> decreased 18% y/y in 2023, driven by a 34% decrease in biodiesel, which was affected by local supply constraints, partly offset by a 1% increase in bioethanol on the
back of higher purchased volumes. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled US$1,491&nbsp;million in 2023 <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-17%</FONT> y/y), mainly due to lower prices of fuels and other refined and petrochemical products, combined with cost pressures in an inflationary context, partially offset by higher processing levels, as well as the new
FX regime that benefited refined and petrochemical exports. It is worth mentioning that Adjusted EBITDA in 4Q23 jumped significantly boosted by a strong recovery in local fuel prices, higher processing levels, the impact of FX devaluation on OPEX
and positive <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-recurring</FONT> results, such as fuels tax refunds and new FX regime for exporters, partially offset by higher imports of fuels. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">9 </P>

</DIV></Center>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit terms, reached
US$11.0/bbl in 2023 aligned with YPF&#146;s historical R&amp;M margins in recent years, but below the US$14.6/bbl reported in 2022. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled
US$1,140&nbsp;million (+36% y/y): 60% refining, 20% midstream oil, 13% logistics and 7% others. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> during 2023 CAPEX was allocated
in the following main projects: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery and a gasoline hydrotreatment plant and revamping of existing gasoline units at La Plata refinery, expected to be operational by Q2 of 2024 and Q4 of 2025, respectively. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

capacity of the Neuquina basin: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">10 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g747204g07h78.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>stood at 294 Kbbl/d in 2023 (+3% y/y), recording a refinery utilization of 90%, on
the back of the revamping of 2 topping units at the La Plata Refinery, completed at the beginning and end of year, which eliminated bottlenecks in the processing of light crude oil, combined with logistics operational optimization and better
availability of crude oil. It is worth noting that in 2023 we achieved the highest crude oil processing level of the last 7 years and the highest production levels of gasoline and middle distillates (diesel and jet fuel) of the last 16 years. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuels sales volumes</B>,<B> </B>in 2023 recorded the highest level ever dispatched in every year, expanding by 3% y/y, with an average market
share of 56.8%, slightly above the previous year (55.5%). It is noteworthy that in 4Q23 fuels demand continued growing, recording a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap"><FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">year-on-year</FONT></FONT> expansion of 9% and 6%
for gasoline and diesel, respectively. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals volumes</B> grew 8% in 2023, due to higher foreign demand, mainly led by methanol exports,
partially offset by lower local demand. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fertilizers, grain and flours sales volumes</B> declined 40% y/y in 2023, as a result of the severe drought
recorded in Argentina in the first half of 2023, partially offset by higher fertilizers demand in local market. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in the
domestic market measured in dollar terms </B>decreased by 6% y/y, as the local fuels adjustments remained below the devaluation of the local currency, particularly affected by the discrete devaluation that took place from <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-August</FONT> to December. However, since November, the company adjusted prices at the pump several times, leading in Q4 to a sequential average increase of 6% in dollar terms, reducing the gap versus import parity
down to 20%. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> declined 18% y/y, aligned with a downward trend in
international prices of petrochemical and certain refined products such as jet fuel and LPG. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.3 GAS&nbsp;&amp; POWER </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$2,374&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-12%</FONT> y/y), mainly due to a 30% lower
price in dollar terms in our subsidiary Metrogas (particularly affected by FX devaluation by <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">year-end),</FONT> slightly offset by a 1% rise in volume sold. Other sales in local currency and gas demand contraction as
producer also impacted the drop in revenues. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas purchases</B> decreased 6% y/y, in line with the decline of natural gas sales, while
<B>operating costs</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other</B> contracted 21% y/y, mainly led by said FX devaluation. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> stood at
US$43&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-70%</FONT> y/y), explained by the contraction of gas prices in dollar terms at our subsidiary Metrogas, and lower demand of natural gas as producer, partly offset by new FX regime for exporters.
</P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">11 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g747204g07h78.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled US$178&nbsp;million (+128% y/y), which continued focusing on the construction of new
midstream gas facilities aiming at <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">de-bottlenecking</FONT> on the potential of the Vaca Muerta formation. In that sense, by the end of 2023 we achieved 93% progress in the revamping of the NGL facility &#147;Tex Loma
La Lata&#148;, which first stage was put into operation in September 2023 and expected to be fully operational by April 2024, adding processing capacity of up to 6 Mm3/d of natural gas and 600 tons/d of NGL in the core hub blocks. In addition, we
continued working on Rinc&oacute;n del Mangrullo gas pipeline expansion project, which reached 94% progress and is expected to be completed by the end of March 2024. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additionally, during 2023 YPF and Petronas completed the feasibility study and the basic engineering of the first phase of LNG project. Also, in 2023, Mega
launched the first stage of its liquid&#146;s business expansion project in Bah&iacute;a Blanca, to increase 20% the propane production capacity by 2025. </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">FCF = Cash flow from Operations less capex (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing
payments (Financing activities). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from operations </B>reached US$5,913&nbsp;million in 2023 (+4% y/y), despite the contraction in
Adjusted EBITDA and the payment of the Maxus settlement agreement (US$287 million), primarily due to higher dividends collections from our subsidiaries, as well as other positive working capital variations, such as the tax credits consumed along the
year generated from an extraordinary income tax prepayment in 2022, the temporary deferred payment of imports of raw material and services, the postponement of employee social security contributions and export duties to 2024, according to the fuels
price agreement implemented by the former Government in Q3, and the differential FX regime for exporters and external financing, in effect until December 2023, in addition to a negative <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-cash</FONT> inventory
variation in 2023. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from investing activities</B> was negative US$5,332&nbsp;million in 2023 compared to negative US$4,016&nbsp;million
in 2022. This increase of 33% was primarily driven by our fully deployed CAPEX plan, almost 36% higher than investments of 2022. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from
financing activities</B> amounted to positive US$278&nbsp;million in 2023, against a negative US$1,227&nbsp;million in the previous year, as the Company continued progressing on its financial plan by securing both local and cross-border
trade-related loans and by tapping the local capital markets. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As a result, the <B>free cash flow</B> for the period was negative by U$740&nbsp;million,
as the continued deployment of our ambitious capex plan, together with our regular interest payments and other expenses were not fully compensated by the cash flow from operations. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of liquidity, our <B>cash and short-term investments</B> stood at US$1,387&nbsp;million by the end of 2023, increasing 27% vs. 2022. We have
continued an active cash management strategy to minimize FX exposure, recording <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-FX</FONT> exposure in 4Q23, down from 8% in 4Q22 and 2% in 3Q23. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">12 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>

<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; margin-left:4%; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Average interest rates for AR$ and US$ debt refer to YPF on a stand-alone basis. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of December&nbsp;31, 2023, YPF&#146;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$6,803&nbsp;million, increasing by US$128&nbsp;million q/q and
US$807&nbsp;million y/y. Despite higher net debt compared to the previous quarter, the Company managed to maintain <B>net leverage ratio</B> around 1.7x. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, the Company completed its financial plan of the year by refinancing all of its trade financing facilities that matured during
4Q23, and also by tapping the local capital market by issuing a dollar-linked <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">3-year</FONT> bond at 0% for US$128&nbsp;million, with a price of US$135.3 per US$100 of nominal value, implying a strongly negative yield
to maturity. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, the company face debt maturities in 2024 for US$1,338&nbsp;million, mainly concentrated in
international bonds, including the final amortization of the 2024 Notes for US$346&nbsp;million and short-term trade facilities for over US$500&nbsp;million. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">On January&nbsp;10, 2024, the company returned to the international capital market, by issuing an export-secured bond for a nominal value of
US$800&nbsp;million dollars. The new bond has a fixed rate of 9.50%, maturing in July 2031 and amortizing in 10 semiannual installments since July 2026. Simultaneously, it was launched a cash tender offer for the 2024 Notes, which resulted in
prepayment of near 40% of the outstanding amount. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The following chart shows our consolidated principal debt maturity profile as of December 2023: </P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. ABOUT YPF </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the largest energy company in
Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce roughly 35% and 30% of the country&#146;s crude oil and natural gas, respectively; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 3
refineries representing approximately 50% of Argentina&#146;s refining capacity and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales with a market share of more than 55%; and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and power, Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes around 25% of
the country&#146;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF is listed in the NYSE and ByMA. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>8. DISCLAIMER </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additional information about YPF S.A., a
sociedad an&oacute;nima organized under the laws of Argentina (the &#147;Company&#148; or &#147;YPF&#148;) can be found in the &#147;Investors&#148; section on the website at <U>www.ypf.com</U>. </P>
